Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. No evidence yet to distinguish body-image/weight concerns from low appetite due to mood or stress, and medical causes are not represented in this psychiatric ICD-10 differential.","is_final":false}

But most mornings I wake up with this kind of heavy, hollow feeling, like the day’s already gone bad before I even put my badge on. It wasn’t one big dramatic thing, but it started after the clinic changed over the EMR and redid the workflow. My hours shifted with all that, and I’ve just sort of been dealing with it since then. It’s been almost two years now, if I’m remembering right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different parts of your life, even when there isn’t an immediate problem?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not across everything, I don’t think. I mean, I worry about work stuff and keeping up, normal things like bills or being on time, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting there worrying about every part of my life for no reason. At least, I haven’t really thought of it that way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than you usually do?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still try to tell myself I could do things, but I don’t really follow through much anymore. I used to run in Van Cortlandt Park, take photos, play chess with friends… normal stuff I actually liked.\n\nLately I’ll think about it and then just kind of go, “What’s the point?” and stay home instead. I end up scrolling on my phone or checking my budget spreadsheet, which sounds productive, I guess, but it’s not really enjoyable. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I do sleep, it’s not like I’m up all night every night, but it’s pretty broken up.\n\nI’ll lie there replaying stuff from work, and I’m kind of listening for noises, if that makes sense. Then I’ll wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning and I can’t really settle back down before the alarm. I don’t know if that counts as insomnia exactly, but it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had unwanted memories of a frightening or disturbing event that keep coming back?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I’ve had upsetting things happen like anyone, but I don’t think I have memories that come back in that way, like I’m reliving something. I tell myself I’m just tired, but honestly I don’t really trust myself to keep up or act normal around people. Does that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have, though I don’t like saying it that way.\n\nIt’s more like I keep thinking I’m being a burden, you know? At the clinic, or even with my family. If I make some small mistake—like if a room turnover gets delayed—I’ll go over it in my head like I ruined everyone’s whole day, like I let everybody down. I know that probably sounds a little dramatic, and I’m not trying to be… but that’s where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: Um… not exactly like I want to do something to myself. I mean… I’ve had upsetting things happen like anyone, but I don’t think I have memories that come back in that way, like I’m reliving something. If I’m understanding what you mean, I don’t think that’s been a big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think so. More than two weeks, definitely.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but the more steady low mood and not really being interested in things… that settled in a while back. I’d say it’s been going on for maybe a year and four months or so. Most days, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still getting to work and handling my day-to-day stuff, so I don’t want to overstate it. But it has been harder.\n\nAt work I’ve been making some mistakes that I really should’ve caught, just avoidable things. And sometimes a coworker will ask me something simple and I have to ask them to repeat it, like I didn’t take it in the first time. That’s embarrassing.\n\nOutside of work, I’ve kind of stopped going to chess nights or doing photography walks. I tell myself I’m just tired, but honestly I don’t really trust myself to keep up or act normal around people. Does that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have, though I don’t like saying it that way.\n\nIt’s more like I keep thinking I’m being a burden, you know? At the clinic, or even with my family. If I make some small mistake—like if a room turnover gets delayed—I’ll go over it in my head like I ruined everyone’s whole day, like I let everybody down. I know that probably sounds a little dramatic, and I’m not trying to be… but that’s where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: Um… not exactly like I want to do something to myself. I haven’t made any kind of plan or anything like that.\n\nBut… yeah, I do catch myself thinking sometimes that it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. But most mornings I wake up with this kind of heavy, hollow feeling, like the day’s already gone bad before I even put my badge on. I don’t know if that counts as depressed, but… it’s been there.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after something particularly stressful happened?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, kind of. It wasn’t one big dramatic thing, but it started after the clinic changed over the EMR and redid the workflow. My hours shifted with all that, and I’ve just sort of been dealing with it since then. It’s been almost two years now, if I’m remembering right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different parts of your life, even when there isn’t an immediate problem?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not across everything, I don’t think. I mean, I worry about work stuff and keeping up, normal things like bills or being on time, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting there worrying about every part of my life for no reason. I mean, I still try to tell myself I could do things, but I don’t really follow through much anymore. I used to run in Van Cortlandt Park, take photos, play chess with friends… normal stuff I actually liked.\n\nLately I’ll think about it and then just kind of go, “What’s the point?” and stay home instead. I end up scrolling on my phone or checking my budget spreadsheet, which sounds productive, I guess, but it’s not really enjoyable. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I do sleep, it’s not like I’m up all night every night, but it’s pretty broken up.\n\nI’ll lie there replaying stuff from work, and I’m kind of listening for noises, if that makes sense. I don’t know if that counts as insomnia exactly, but it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had unwanted memories of a frightening or disturbing event that keep coming back?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I’ve had upsetting things happen like anyone, but I don’t think I have memories that come back in that way, like I’m reliving something. If I’m understanding what you mean, I don’t think that’s been a big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think so. More than two weeks, definitely.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but the more steady low mood and not really being interested in things… that settled in a while back. I’d say it’s been going on for maybe a year and four months or so. At the clinic, or even with my family. If I make some small mistake—like if a room turnover gets delayed—I’ll go over it in my head like I ruined everyone’s whole day, like I let everybody down. I know that probably sounds a little dramatic, and I’m not trying to be… but that’s where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: Um… not exactly like I want to do something to myself. I haven’t made any kind of plan or anything like that.\n\nBut… yeah, I do catch myself thinking sometimes that it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Or if I could just disappear for a while and not have to be… here, dealing with everything. I’m sorry, I don’t mean to make that sound alarming. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do these symptoms feel mainly connected to the changes at work?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah… mostly. It’s been… almost two years now, I guess.
